California Masonic Memorial Temple – San Francisco

i

DAVID C. DECKER
Grand Master
October 11, 2000 – October 10, 2001
ii

DAVID C. DECKER
Grand Master – 2000-2001
Our Grand Master is a member of Upland-Mt. Baldy Lodge No. 419 where he
served as Master in 1974. In January 1996, he was presented with the Hiram Award.
He has been married to his wife, Nancy, for thirty-nine years.
Most Worshipful Brother Decker was born in Illinois on April 4, 1937. He
attended public schools in Ladora, Iowa, and continued his education at Chaffey College
and San Bernardino Valley College.
He retired from GTE after 30 years of service.
David and Nancy are members of the First United Methodist Church in
Riverside.
His hobbies are golf, traveling (especially cruises), good music and working in
stained glass.
Brother Decker is a member of several Masonic organizations including the
Santa Ana Scottish Rite where he is a 33rd Degree Elect, Riverside York Rite, Al
Malaikah Shrine Temple where he serves as an Ambassador at Large, National
Sojourners, Grotto, Sciots, Order of the Amaranth, Order of the Eastern Star, Royal
Order of Scotland, the Riverside High Twelve Club, and the Red Cross of Constantine.
He serves on the Board of the Shrine Hospital in Los Angeles.
Most Worshipful Brother Decker served as Inspector of the 606th Masonic
District from 1986 through 1991. He served Grand Lodge with distinction as Senior
Grand Deacon under Most Worshipful Stanley M. Cazneaux, Grand Master in 1991-92.
He was named a Trustee of the Board of Trustees of the California Masonic
Foundation where he served as Vice President in 1992-93, and the President from 1993
to November 1997.
He has served on the Nob Hill Masonic Management, Inc. Board of Trustees,
the California Masonic Memorial Temple Board, California Masonic Foundation,
Masonic Homes Board, Wardens’ Leadership Retreat Committee and the Executive
Committee.
In 1998, Brother Decker was honored by the International Supreme Council,
Order of DeMolay, with the Honorary Legion of Honor.

I, JOHN L. COOPER III, Grand Secretary of the MW Grand Lodge of Free
and Accepted Masons of the State of California, do hereby certify that the
following pages contain a true and faithful transcript of the proceedings of that
body at its One Hundred Fifty-Second Annual Communication, commencing at
the City of San Francisco, Monday the Eighth of October, A.L. 6001 and
terminating on Wednesday the Tenth of October, A.L. 6001.

I have hereunto appended my official signature and have affixed the seal of the
MW Grand Lodge of Free and Accepted Masons of the State of California, at
the office of the Grand Secretary, this First day of January, in the year of Light,
two thousand and two.

Grand Secretary

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

3

PUBLIC OPENING CEREMONY
Sunday, October 7, 2001
2:00 PM
The Public Opening of Grand Lodge was held in the Masonic
Auditorium of the California Masonic Memorial Temple, 1111 California Street,
San Francisco, California beginning at 2:00 p.m. Grand Master David C.
Decker, accompanied by his Grand Lodge Officers, entered the Auditorium to
the sound of trumpets and organ, and assumed their stations. The Grand
Chaplain gave the invocation.
The Grand Master welcomed all in attendance to the Public Opening of
nd
the 152 Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of California. He thanked
Brother Raymond W. Peltier, Chairman of the Youth Orders Committee, for
raising the Grand Lodge.
The Grand Master then noted that the gavel used by Brother Peltier to
raise the Grand Lodge had first been presented to him on January 11, 1974 when
he was installed Master of Upland-Mt. Baldy Lodge No. 419. Grand Master
Decker also noted that the Sciots Traveling Gavel, also being used today, was
crafted by Senior Warden Rusty Smith of Irving Lodge No. 1218, Irving, Texas,
and is meant for the furtherment of Masonic friendship between Lodges. The
gavel started its journey by being used to open and close the January 1998 stated
meeting of Irving Lodge. It traveled briefly in Texas in early 1998, in April
1998, the gavel was used in Istanbul, Turkey as the General Assembly of the
Grand Lodge of Turkey, and in December 1998, it was back in Texas. It then
traveled to Arkansas, Montana, Pennsylvania and New Jersey before being
presented to the Grand Master in November 2000. The Grand Master read a list
of 64 Lodges and Masonic organizations within our California Family of
Freemasonry where this gavel has been used during the last year. The Grand
Master announced that this gavel would continue its travels and at the close of
this Annual Communication it would be given to the Grand Master of Texas to
take back with him.
The Grand Master then displayed a beautiful gavel given to him by
Roseville Lodge No. 222 in Antelope, California on June 22, 2001. The gavel
was handcrafted by Brother Bob Henson. He also displayed a gavel made from
th
orange wood presented to him at the 240 Masonic District Reception in
Placerville, California on June 23, 2001.
The Grand Marshal was then asked to retire to escort into the
auditorium, the leaders of the concordant and appendant bodies of the Family of
Freemasonry in California. The following were present:

The Grand Master expressed to each of the representatives of the
concordant and appendant bodies the appreciation of all Masons in California
because of their support for Masonic principles, and also for the magnificent
charities each group supports and represents.
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to escort the Presiding
Officers of the General York Rite Bodies to the East for a special presentation.
Most Excellent Sam Pitassi expressed the appreciation of the York Rite Bodies
for the Grand Master’s support this year. He then presented the Grand Master
with a beautiful handmade clock.
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to direct the guests to
the seats reserved for them in the auditorium.
The Grand Master asked the Grand Marshal to escort Brother William
J. Bray III, Worthy Grand Patron of the Grand Chapter of California, Order of

6

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

the Eastern Star, to the East, and the Grand Master asked him to bring the
greetings on behalf of all the members of the Family of Freemasonry in
California.
Brother Bray noted that during the year the Order of the Eastern Star
has stressed the importance of a strong and united Masonic Family.
Emphasizing the importance of unity among the concordant and appendant
bodies, he complimented the Grand Master and his lady, Nancy, for laying the
cornerstone of Freemasonry for the new millennium. He concluded with wishes
for a productive and successful Grand Lodge session.
The Grand Master thanked Brother Bray for his remarks, and asked the
Grand Marshal to conduct Brother Bray to a seat in the auditorium.
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to retire and escort to
a place west of the Altar the adult leaders of our Masonic Youth Groups in
California, and the elected youth leaders of the Order of DeMolay, the
International Order of Job’s Daughters, and the International Order of Rainbow
for Girls. The following persons were introduced:
Mrs. Sara Lee Martin ............................................. Supreme Deputy in California
International Order of Rainbow for Girls
Mrs. Kathe Osburn ............................................................ Grand Guardian of the
State of California, International Order of Job’s Daughters

State of California, International Order of Job’s Daughters

Mr. John James .......................................................................... Master Councilor
Jurisdiction of Southern California, Order of DeMolay

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

7

The Grand Master then said, “Brethren and Ladies, you all hold a
special place in my heart.” Because of them, the leaders of tomorrow who will
hold the future of the nation, are in their hands. He expressed the appreciation of
the Grand Lodge of California for their leadership in the Masonic Youth Groups,
and for all the work the adult leaders and the elected youth leaders do on behalf
of their organizations.
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to conduct the adult
leaders of the Masonic Youth Groups to seats in the auditorium, along with other
special guests, and then to conduct the four elected youth leaders from California
to seats in the East.
Throughout the remainder of the afternoon’s program, the Grand
Master called on each of the elected youth leaders in turn for their very special
remarks. Each speaker was well-received with applause from the audience.
Each elected youth leader presented a special gift to the Grand Master.
The visiting dignitaries from other Grand Jurisdictions were then
escorted into the auditorium, accompanied by the flag of his state or nation. The
flags were presented by the San Francisco Scottish Rite Knights of St. Andrew.
The Grand Master invited the audience to take special note of the significance of
each flag as it was presented, described and posted. The Grand Master praised
the flags as symbols of freedom and independence, and called on those
assembled to consider how privileged we are to inhabit a land that is so unique,
so meaningful, and so free.
The parade of flags was most dramatic and inspiring, accompanied by
an informative dramatic narration describing the history of each flag as it was
carried into the auditorium.
The Grand Marshal then introduced the visiting Grand Masters,
representatives of other Grand Masters, and visitors from other jurisdictions:
W\ David H. Luebke ........................................................ Senior Grand Steward

The Grand Master graciously welcomed the distinguished guests and on
behalf of the Masons of California expressed our appreciation for them having
joined us. The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to afford the guests
seats in the auditorium.
The Grand Master then directed the Grand Marshal to retire and present
the Flag of Our Country before the Altar of Freemasonry. The flag was
presented at the Altar by California Commandery No. 1, Knights Templar.
The Grand Master then invited all citizens to join him in the Pledge of
Allegiance to the Flag of Our Country and the singing of “God Bless America”.
The Grand Master then called upon Brother Raymond W. Peltier,
Chairman of the Grand Lodge Youth Orders Committee to assist him in
presenting the Grand Master’s Youth Achievement Award to this year’s four
honorees. The award recognizes singular service to the young people in the
Masonic Youth Orders.
The Grand Master asked the Grand Marshal to escort the recipients to a
place West of the Altar. The Grand Master and the Deputy Grand Master then
presented the awards while Brother Peltier read the biography of the following
honorees:

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

9

Worshipful James H. Strehlow, Island City Lodge No. 215
Worshipful Strehlow is a Senior DeMolay.
Lodge No. 215 in 1979 and served as Master in 1985.

He joined Island City

Although Worshipful Jim has no children he actively supports the
Masonic Youth Orders, but particularly DeMolay. Because of Worshipful Jim’s
efforts, along with two other advisors, Albany Chapter of DeMolay is open
today. Worshipful Jim is currently Dad Advisor of the Chapter.
In addition to his activity with Albany Chapter he has served many
years as a member of the evaluation staff of the NORCAL P.R.I.D.E. Ritual
Tournament and the PAC-WEST Regional Ritual Competition.
Masonry and DeMolay are extremely important to Jim as he is truly
dedicated to these fraternities and continues to support them in every way that he
can.
Congratulations on receiving the Grand Master’s Youth Support
Award.
Brother William Caldwell, Sotoyome-Curtis Lodge No. 123
Worshipful Bill joined Sotoyome-Curtis Lodge No. 123 in 1966. He
served as Master of the Lodge in 1972. In 1996, he was honored with the Hiram
Award and in the year 2000 he was named Mason of the Year. Worshipful Bill
is a member of the Santa Rosa Valley Scottish Rite and the Aahmes Shrine
Temple. He is also a Past Patron of the Order of the Eastern Star.
Bill was active in the Healdsburg Chapter of DeMolay during the
1960’s and 1970’s until it closed. He is now involved with the Santa Rosa
Chapter where his two grandsons are members. Grandson Nathan is Senior
Councilor-Elect and grandson Will is Junior Councilor-Elect.
Bill has served Job’s Daughters as Associate Bethel Guardian of Santa
Rosa Bethel where two granddaughters were members.
One of the
granddaughters is a Past Honored Queen.
Bill has been active with the Healdsburg Rainbow Assembly since 1970
where he has served on the Advisory Board since that time. He has also been
Rainbow Dad more times than can be remembered. He is the Rainbow liaison to
the lodges in the area. Worshipful Bill has served on the Grand Assembly
Scholarship Committee since 1977 and is currently Chairman of the committee.
Today we are pleased to present to you the Grand Master’s Youth
Support Award. Congratulations.

10

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Worshipful Charles R. Beaumont, Claremont Lodge No. 436
Worshipful Beaumont is a Senior DeMolay. He joined DeMolay in
Washington D.C. while in high school. Worshipful Charles is a Past Master
Councilor.
Worshipful Charlie was raised in B.B. French Lodge in Washington
D.C. in 1945. He later moved to California and affiliated with Claremont Lodge
No. 436 in 1967 and served the lodge as Master in 1972. Worshipful Beaumont
received the Hiram Award last year.
Worshipful Charlie has served DeMolay for over 30 years. He has
been on the Advisory Councils of both San Bernardino and Claremont Chapters
where he has served as Scribe Advisor and Board Chairman. He has also served
DeMolay at the League and Supreme levels and is currently a Member Emeritus
of the International Council Order of DeMolay.
Worshipful Charles has received many honors from DeMolay, among
them are the Cross of Honor, Legion of Honor and the Guild of the Leather
Apron. He is a Past Dean of the Southern California Preceptory of the Legion of
Honor and is presently their Secretary.
Two of his sons are Past Master Councilors and his granddaughter is a
Past Honored Queen of Bethel 301, Claremont.

Worshipful Floyd is also being awarded the Grand Master’s Youth

the presentation at a later time.
The distinguished Masons were saluted with the Public Grand Honors.
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to conduct the recipients to
seats in the auditorium.
The Grand Master then spoke of the vital importance of the Masonic
Youth Groups in their influence for good in this era of challenges facing our
youth. He emphasized that we cannot underestimate the important role of the
Masonic youth organizations in developing the lives of tomorrow’s business
professionals, government officials, parents, and religious leaders.
The Grand Master then announced that he is directing the funds from
the Grand Master’s Special Project to the leadership programs maintained by
each of the Masonic Youth Groups. He noted that during the year he had the

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

11

opportunity to present checks to the youth groups from the California Masonic
Foundation.
During the afternoon program the Grand Master called upon each of the
four elected youth leaders for their remarks. They all delivered inspiring
messages to those assembled in the auditorium. As each youth leader favored us
with his or her remarks, the Grand Master presented each one with a check for
leadership training from the Grand Lodge of California. Each youth leader in
turn thanked the Grand Master and presented him with a special gift.
The Grand Master then introduced a special presentation on the
Masonic Student Assistance Program. He noted that this program was adopted
by the California Masonic Foundation during the period that he had served as
President of the Foundation.
The Grand Master noted that the Masonic Student Assistance Program
seeks to enhance the skills and linkages of existing human resources in the
schools and their local community. The objective is early and effective
intervention with students demonstrating unusual behavior, which could threaten
their success at school and be indicators of problems associated with substance
abuse.
The Grand Master added that since its inception in 1994 under Most
Worshipful William F. Stovall, Past Grand Master, the Masonic Student
Assistance Program has trained nearly 3,000 California educators from 781
school districts. As a result nearly 25,000 California students have been
identified and referred for assistance.
The Grand Master then introduced the two men who founded and
continue to teach this national program, Brothers Larry Newman and Thom
Stecher, the founders of Newman Stecher.
Each Brother in his remarks gave a heartfelt description of the absolute
need for programs such as the Masonic Student Assistance Program, and of the
good that the program has provided for countless children and families. Their
remarks were received with thunderous applause from those assembled in the
auditorium. The Grand Master thanked them for their inspirational words.
The Grand Master then spoke of how on September 11 our world was
changed forever, when terrorists executed a horrid plan to hijack domestic
aircraft and crash them into the two towers of the World Trade Center in New
York City and into the Pentagon near Washington, DC.
The Grand Master acknowledged the heroism and courage of the
hundreds of New York City firefighters and police officers who gave their lives
for the rescue of the innocent. He noted that nineteen firefighters from the San
Francisco Fire Department had volunteered to assist in the New York City relief
efforts.

12

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

In recognition of the San Francisco firefighters, the Grand Master then
called on the Grand Marshal to escort members of the San Francisco Fire
Department to a place west of the altar. The firefighters were Lieutenant Daniel
V. Armenta, Acting Captain Patrick Gardner, and Lieutenant Victor H. Wyrsch.
The Grand Master led a standing ovation for the firefighters and in brief
remarks saluted them for their actions “above and beyond the call of duty”, that
were emblematic of the spirit of America.
The Grand Master asked the Grand Marshal to conduct the firefighters
to the East. Lieutenant Armenta gave some brief remarks, and the Grand Master
then presented the firefighters with a plaque of recognition.
The Grand Master then announced that he had authorized an additional
donation of $5,000 to the World Trade Center Relief Fund administered by the
Grand Lodge of New York to be made in the name and in honor of the nineteen
San Francisco firefighters who participated in the relief effort.
The Grand Master then asked those assembled to stand for a moment of
silence in memory of those who lost their lives as a result of this tragedy.
The Grand Chaplain prayed:
“Great and merciful Father, we remember today the lives of
thousands who were lost in a horrible act of hate. Those who
are now committed to your hands, were mothers and fathers,
daughters and sons. They had so much left to do, Lord, but on
the earth they have made their mark. Accept them now into
your kingdom and bestow upon them your grace that they may
know peace and love everlasting. Amen.”
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to conduct the
firefighters to seats in the auditorium.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

VERNE WALDEN HENDRIX
Past Grand Secretary
1914-2001
Verne Walden Hendrix, a Past Grand Secretary of this Grand Lodge,
came into this world on December 18, 1914 in a log house in the small
community of Roundup, Montana. He departed this world and entered his
Father’s house on February 27, 2001 in Walnut Creek, California – a life span of
86 years, 2 months and 9 days.
Verne, the second child of Edgar and Anna Hendrix, grew to adulthood
in Long Beach, California to which city the family had moved during the great
depression, and because of that event he recognized early the value of the
American work ethic. Graduating from high school in 1932 and junior college in
1934, he then attended the University of California at Berkeley living at the
International House. After graduating with a degree in Business Administration
in 1937 he took post-graduate work in law at Boalt Hall, following that with
courses at Hastings School of Law.
Verne met his future wife, Ruth, at a “Big Game” party in 1938, and in
October of the following year, the two were married. From this union three
children – two boys and one girl – were born to this happy couple. Verne was
devoted to Ruth, a devotion that encompassed 61 years. During these years they
shared many joys as well as some sorrows. Perhaps the greatest sorrow they
shared was the untimely death of their daughter, Lynn, who passed away in 1985
at the age of 42. Both Ruth and Verne recently agreed “it’s been a great trip for
over 61 years.”
This kind, compassionate and dedicated man had three great loves – his
family, his church and his Masonry. He also loved the high mountains and was
an ardent back packer and camper and above all an avid fisherman.
Verne was involved in several civic activities. He was a member of the
first Board of Directors of the Diablo Valley YMCA; a troop leader for two
separate Boy Scout troops; the area representative to the United Crusades; and a
member of various boards and committees of his church, Hillcrest
Congregational United Church of Christ where he served as moderator.
Prior to his appointment as the Assistant Grand Secretary of the Grand
Lodge, Verne was employed by Dunn and Bradstreet as an investigator; by
Standard Oil Company in the accounting department; and by Golden State
Creamery in the personnel department. He as also employed by the Harris
Manufacturing Company of Stockton, California again in the personnel
department and by the Waterfront Employers Association, where he was in
charge of the weekly payroll for all Pacific Maritime waterfront employees. He
also served in conjunction with that job as a representative on the FBI panel
which was engaged in checking for subversive activities on the waterfront.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

19

During the years of his employment, Verne applied for and received the
degrees of Masonry in Mount Diablo Lodge No. 448, being raised to the sublime
degree of Master Mason on August 15, 1950. He served as Master of that Lodge
in 1957. He held membership in Concord Chapter, Order of the Eastern Star.
He was President of the East Bay Masters and Wardens Association, comprising
63 Lodges. He was President of Mount Diablo Temple Board and was
appointed Junior Grand Deacon by the late Past Grand Master, Albert Breslauer.
In 1961 he was appointed Assistant Grand Secretary of the Grand Lodge. After
serving in that capacity for 17 years, Verne was elected in 1978 to the office of
Grand Secretary. He also served as a member of two Grand Lodge committees –
Policy and General Purposes and Laymen’s Night Speakers Panel.
Verne was the recipient of several Masonic awards – his Lodge’s “Man
of the Year”, the DeMolay Honorary Legion of Honor, the George C. Johnson
Award, again from his Lodge, the Scottish Rite 33E degree, the Hiram Award
and last year the Golden Veterans’ Award.
I came to know Verne as perhaps, few outside of his family knew him.
For 10 years we had an extremely close relationship both in the Grand Lodge
office and in the field as we traveled from one end of our jurisdiction to the other
end, conducting Lodge Secretaries’ meetings and participating in other Masonic
events. This good brother had a genuine appreciation and understanding of the
true meaning of the tenets of Masonry, which he continually practiced on a dayto-day basis.
Verne’s work is now completed. He has been laid to rest. “The silver
cord is loosed, the golden bowl is broken, the pitcher is broken at the fountain
and the wheel is broken at the cistern. The dust has returned to the earth as it
was and the spirit has returned to God who gave it.”

20

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

THEODORE MERIAM
GRAND MASTER: 1964-1965
Born: April 18, 1910
Entered Into Rest: August 5, 2001
I consider it a great honor to be asked by our Grand Master to present a
eulogy to Past Grand Master Ted Meriam. I refer to him as “Ted”, rather than
the more formal “Theodore” because that was always his preference. It was part
of his nature, part of his common touch.
Whenever one of our Past Grand Masters comes to the end of his
earthly journey, we all feel a sense of real loss, but I must admit that in the case
of Ted Meriam, I feel a much more personal loss than usual. It was fifty-seven
years ago this past month, that I went to work at M. Oser & Co. where Ted
served as manager. Ted usually had young men from Chico State College, but
this was wartime and there were few young men left on campus. So as a
seventeen-year old young man, it was my privilege to get to know Ted Meriam
early in my life. I was fortunate to have Ted as one of my role models in that
formative period of my life.
However, when one comes to paying appropriate tribute to Ted, of
necessity, it must involve a great deal more than personal recollections. Over a
year ago, I made a fortuitous decision. I began saving material from local
sources about Ted. These were much too voluminous to include in this
presentation, but let me just review some of these. I could show you the program
when Ted was awarded the “Distinguished Eagle Scout Award”, the only person
ever to receive such an award in the Northern part of our state. I could quote
from the program of April 25, 2000, “A Tribute to Ted Meriam and a
Celebration of the Great Bible of 1498”. This was held at the Meriam Library
on the campus of California State University, Chico. On October 19, 1999, Ted
was honored by the Chico Rotary Club, “A Life of Service Above Self” This
program is significant because it lists Ted’s record of service as follows:

·

1947-48, President, Chico Rotary Club; 1951, Paul Harris Fellow,
Community Service Award.

·

1947-1959 Chico City Council; Mayor 1949-1959. (I point out
that Ted served five consecutive two-year terms as Mayor; no
mayor since has served more than one term.)

·

California State University: Advisory Board, 1950-60; again 197080

·

California State University System, Trustee 1960-70; Chairman
1968-69

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

21

·

Grand Master, Grand Lodge of California, Free and Accepted
Mason 1964-65

·

Enloe Hospital Board 1969-1977; Chairman 1972-1977

·

California Historical Society; Book Club of California; Book Club
of Sacramento

·

Bidwell Mansion Association; 30 year member and Founding
member

·

Stansbury Home Preservation Society—Founding Member

·

Association for Northern California Records and Research—
Founding Member

·

Salvation Army; Advisory Board Member

·

Stanford Alumni Association – Life Member 1931

·

Boy Scouts of America; Troop 3 Chico, and life-long supporter.
He was also awarded the Silver Beaver Award for his long service
to Scouting.

I have edited this list for the sake of brevity.
Let me refer to the news story from the Chico Enterprise-Record telling
of Ted’s death, with the heading: “A city full of reminders,” with subtitles, “Ted
Meriam played a key role in shaping Chico”, and “MERIAM: Service to his
hometown was a priority.” In that article, Glenn Kendall, retired President of
Chico State, described Meriam as follows: “Ted was a quiet, gentle person. Not
aggressive, but always very thoughtful about his program. He has the respect of
all the people in the community.”
We also have a weekly newspaper in Chico, The Chico News and
Review, which generally presents points of view well left of center. Ted was
probably rarely in agreement with that paper’s politics, although he always read
it thoroughly. However, its report following Ted’s funeral was headed, “A man
in love” with the subtitle, “For a half century Ted Meriam personified leadership
and service in Chico.” The article concluded, “…there is no replacing Ted
Meriam in the hearts of Chicoans. Long ago we gave him the only nickname
that fully suited him, and it’s a name that will remain his for as long as this town
survives: Mr. Chico”.
The Chico Enterprise-Record, on August 8, 2001 dedicated its editorial
to Ted Meriam. I quote briefly from that, “To call him a statesman – far
different from the connotations of a politician – brings to mind images of
wisdom, poise, and a viewpoint sufficiently broad to see the other side.” The
editorial goes on to reveal of side of Ted that we in Grand Lodge are fully
familiar with, “Those who knew him as a friend or acquaintance knew they were

22

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

in the presence of a great man, but they also knew that he would scoff at that
inference.”
You may have noticed that I have mentioned very little about Ted’s
Masonic activities or his great leadership as Grand Master in 1964-65. It is
difficult for any Past Grand Master to concede that a man’s other
accomplishments might tend to overshadow his service as Grand Master.
However, this seems to be true in Ted’s case. Indeed, the obituary in the local
paper, listed his numerous accomplishments and honors and concluded, almost
as an afterthought, “in addition he was grand master of Mason in California.”
We, in this Grand Lodge, know that he not only served as an outstanding Grand
Master, but continued his service for many years---ten years as Chairman of
Policy and General Purposes, and his service on Jurisprudence. His most recent
service was as a member of the Masonic History Committee. His great
knowledge and interest in history contributed greatly to the recently published
book celebrating the sesquicentennial observance of the Grand Lodge of
California.
I conclude this eulogy with reference to our coffee group. It was a
diverse gathering. Ted and I were the only Masons. Others included a retired
Vice-President of the Bank of America, a devout Mormon; a retired Journalism
professor, an Irish Catholic; an artist with Sicilian background who grew up on
the streets of New York, a lapsed Catholic; a Political Scientist, who once served
as an aide to Governor Pat Brown, a member of Grace Community Church; the
retired Graduate Manager from Chico State, who had ties to the Salvation Army.
I rounded out this ecumenical picture as an Episcopalian. Ted referred to this
group as “The Council on World Affairs.” You would be surprised at the
problems we solved. However, we were too late to save Richard Nixon, and Bill
Clinton wouldn’t listen to us. There is no doubt that Ted Meriam was the
patriarch of the group, and he would often call us to attention, and tell us “I want
to share this with you.” He would proceed to read something that he had picked
from the New Yorker, The Rotarian, and occasionally from The California
Freemason. Some of these anecdotes were profound; some just amusing, but all
of us were very enriched by this experience.
I now conclude knowing full well that I have probably not done justice
to Ted. But “my brethren, let us not forget that we too are mortal and that our
spirits too must return to the God that spake them into existence…” And when
our time comes leave this earth that we may have, at least in some small degree,
lived our lives as did Ted Meriam.

WILLIAM HENRY PRICE IV
GRAND MASTER: 1972-1973
Born: June 21, 1922
Entered Into Rest: September 4, 2001
William Henry Price IV exemplified the principles of a Master Mason
in all his undertakings. He served this Grand Lodge as Grand Master in 19721973 and continued to serve Freemasonry in the later years. He had an abiding
faith in the fraternity and took every opportunity to extol its virtues and practice
its teachings.
Our brother was born in Cumberland, Maryland on June 21, 1922.
From his humble beginnings, as the son of a builder of roadways, he and his
parents often lived in a tent at the construction sites. He was educated in the
public schools of Fremont, Ohio and later attended Bowling Green University.
In 1946 he became associated with Bowes Seal-Fast Corporation, a
manufacturer and distributor of automotive parts and accessories in Indianapolis,
Indiana. Two years later he was transferred to Riverside, California to manage
the Riverside plant. He spent the rest of his working career in Riverside and
became President and General Manager of the Riverside facility as well as a
Vice President and Director of the parent Corporation.
While taking flying lessons in Indianapolis, he met and married his
lovely wife, Jane DuPuis, and they became the proud parents of a daughter,
Candace. Her marriage to Jeff Gunther provided a grandson, Matthew, who was
also Brother Bill’s pride and joy.
Our distinguished brother was raised to the sublime degree of Master
Mason in Evergreen Lodge no. 259, Riverside, in 1950, at a time when
membership was increasing rapidly. His talents became apparent to the Lodge
officers and he was appointed Chaplain in 1954 and elected Junior Warden the
following year. He served as Worshipful Master in 1959. Only three years later,
th
he was appointed Inspector of the 616 Masonic District and held that position
until 1966 when he was appointed Assistant Grand Lecturer. After another three
years, he was selected by the Committee for the Selection of Junior Grand
Warden as the recommendee for that position. He was elected and installed
Junior Grand Warden of the Grand Lodge of California and Hawaii in 1969. He
served with great distinction in the progressive line of Grand Lodge officers and
was elected and installed as Grand Master in October 1972.
Our Most Worshipful Brother was a member of many Masonic
affiliated organizations, including the York Rite, the Long Beach Scottish Rite in
which he was coroneted an Honorary Inspector General of the 33E, and the Al
Malaikah and El Bekal Shrine Temples.

26

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

In his ceremonial duties, he had the honor and pleasure of sharing the
podium with then Governor Ronald Reagan at a cornerstone ceremony for a
public school in Orange County.
After completing his successful term as Grand Master, he later served
on the Policy and General Purposes Committee of the Grand Lodge where his
wise counsel was effectively applied to the responses of this Committee to the
numerous Resolutions proposed by the members of Grand Lodge. He singlehandedly researched the Proceedings of the Grand Lodge for past decades and
compiled a valuable reference index of the issues presented and the actions taken
in former years.
In addition to his Masonic career, he was an ordained Elder of the
Calvary Presbyterian Church in Riverside for many years, served on the Board
of Directors of Riverside Community Hospital and was a member of the Victoria
Country Club where he played a very respectable game of golf.
Most Worshipful Brother Price was an accomplished musician, having
mastered the flute, clarinet and electronic organ. As his next door neighbor for
30 years, we were often treated to his musical renditions. During his three years
of service in the United States Army as Personnel Sergeant Major, he organized
a dance band for the entertainment of the military personnel.
We are indeed fortunate to have enjoyed many years of service and
leadership under Most Worshipful William H. Price and we are profoundly
grateful for his influence while here on earth.
The brittle thread which bound him to earth has been severed but the
memory of his presence will remain with us always.

Election of Officers
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Secretary to explain the
balloting procedure for the election of officers. No ballot would be necessary if
the office were not contested. If more than one nomination were received for an
office, ballot cards would be distributed for the purpose of balloting for that
office.

Election of the Grand Master
Deputy Grand Master C. Ray Whitaker was nominated Grand Master
by Most Worshipful Stanley L. Channon, Past Grand Master. No further
nominations appearing, it was moved, seconded and carried that the nominations
be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot
for Right Worshipful C. Ray Whitaker to serve as Grand Master for the ensuing
Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was declared unanimously elected.

Election of Deputy Grand Master
Senior Grand Warden M. William Holsinger was nominated Deputy
Grand Master by Most Worshipful Alvin J. Weis, Past Grand Master. No further
nominations appearing, it was moved, seconded and carried that the nominations
be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot
for Right Worshipful M. William Holsinger to serve as Deputy Grand Master for
the ensuing Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was declared
unanimously elected.

40

unanimous ballot for Right Worshipful Howard D. Kirkpatrick to serve as Senior
Grand Warden for the ensuing Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was
declared unanimously elected.

carried that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot for
Worshipful Brother David Richard Doan to serve as Junior Grand Warden for

explain the balloting procedure for the written ballot.

Grand Lecturer and instructed the delegates present to mark their ballots for the

tellers.

counting he was appointing Most Worshipful Charles H. Alexander, Past Grand
Master, as the Supervisor of Elections. He requested Most Worshipful Brother
Alexander to meet the tellers in Meeting Room Number 1 behind the stage and
supervise the counting of the ballots, and when counted, report the results to the
Grand Lodge.

42

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

After determining that all had cast ballots, the Grand Master declared
the ballot closed. The Grand Master called the Grand Lodge free from restraint
until the sound of the gavel in the East.
After explaining the procedure used to count the ballots, Most
Worshipful Charles H. Alexander reported that Worshipful Brother Harold A.
Macy had received a majority of the votes cast. Upon Worshipful Brother
Macy’s acceptance, the Grand Master declared him elected.
The Grand Master then named Ulysses G. Patropulos as Grand Lecturer
Emeritus.

Grand Lodge Called from Labor to Refreshment
The Grand Master then called Grand Lodge from labor to refreshment
for the purpose of installing the Grand Lodge Officers for the ensuing Grand
Lodge Year. The Installation was announced as being at 11:00 a.m. on
Wednesday, October 10, 2001.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

47

GRAND MASTER’S MESSAGE
My Brethren:
This report is in compliance with Section 1060 of the California
Masonic Code which requires the Grand Master to “Present, at each Annual
Communication, a written message setting forth: (1) all his official acts during
the year; (2) the general condition of Masonry within the jurisdiction; and (3)
recommending such legislation as he deems necessary or expedient for the
welfare of the Craft.”
As I assumed the office of Grand Master one year ago, I knew the task
before me would not be an easy one, and I leave this office with mixed emotions.
I’ve had to make some very difficult decisions and I know I have had the
support of this Grand Lodge. I have always believed that if I made decisions
that were well thought out and stayed within the bounds of our Fraternity, I could
go to sleep at night knowing I had done what was right. I must tell you, my
Brethren, I never shied away from the problems that confront each Grand Master
as he proceeds through his year.
Several months ago I read an article in the Scottish Rite Journal which
included the following:

“Your task is to build a better world,” said God, and I
questioned, “How? This world is such a vast place and, oh, so
complicated now. And I am so small and useless, there is
nothing I can do.” But God, in all His wisdom, said, “You just
build a better you.”

I firmly believe all we must do to build a better fraternity is as God
said, “You just build a better you.”
When I was elected Junior Grand Warden in 1997, I asked myself what
could I bring to this Grand Lodge? How could we get our fraternity back on
track to move forward in the New Millennium? I have attempted to apply my
skills from management in the telephone company to the “job” of Grand Master.
It wasn’t very long before I learned a plan had been established as
recommended by our Research and Planning Committee. I took that plan,
updated it, and worked it to make sure it was followed during this past year.
That plan has been revised each of the last two years to bring it more in line with
today’s Masonry. It is my pleasure to report on each of the priorities and update
you on their current status.

48

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Priority No. 1 – To be well governed, organized and managed.
A new system of oversight for Grand Lodge committees was instituted
this year when the Deputy Grand Master, the Senior Grand Warden and the
Junior Grand Warden were assigned specific committees to supervise. This
encouraged committee chairmen to achieve the goals which they had set for their
committees, and to report through the Executive Committee their achievements.
It is recommended this new procedure be continued in the future. This system
furthered Priority No. 1, to be well governed, organized and managed.

Priority No. 2 – To be well led, with many opportunities to learn and to become
a leader.
The Wardens’ Leadership Retreats continued to look for ways to
improve our leadership training. This year 193 lodges participated in the Senior
Wardens’ Retreats, and 184 in the Junior Wardens’ Retreats. In addition, we
presented six Officers’ Management Workshops, and six Secretaries’
Administrative Workshops. All were designed to further Priority No. 2, to be
well led, with many opportunities to learn and to become a leader.

Priority No. 3 – To attract, satisfy, develop and retain our members.
This priority was largely achieved through the activities of the
Membership Development Committee, under the leadership of Worshipful
Brother Paul McCleary of San Diego. The website received recorded inquiries
from 228 men interested in becoming Masons. Our ability to retain our members
was measured by the number of suspensions for non-payment of dues, and our
Grand Secretary reported there were 1,399 suspensions for non-payment of dues
– 215 more than in the previous year.

Priority No. 4 – To be a fraternity that makes good men better, with members
who understand and practice the tenets of Freemasonry.
This year was the first year of the mandatory Basic Education Program
for our candidates. Copies of the three Candidate Education Booklets were
distributed to all lodges. We also instituted a pilot program to encourage the
retention and advancement of candidates, the Mentoring Program. It is too early
to judge the results of the pilot program. It was sufficiently successful that we
published a Mentoring Manual, available for purchase from the Grand Lodge
office and available free on our Grand Lodge Members Internet Site,
freemason.org. However, it should be noted that although we Initiated 74 more
men this year than last, we Passed to the Degree of Fellow Craft 83 fewer
Entered Apprentice Masons than the year before, and Raised 73 less men to the
Sublime Degree of Master Mason. That’s a trend in the wrong direction. My

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

49

hope is that these candidates knew a lot more about Masonry this year than last
since most of them had to complete an open-book test on Freemasonry before
they could take the next degree.

Priority No. 5 – To be a relevant, preeminent, member-centered organization of
men of high quality.
It is difficult to ascertain whether or not we achieved our Fifth Priority.
That really can only be measured by our lodges themselves. I suggest our
Grand Lodge leadership consider ways next year to measure the satisfaction of
our members so that we will have a better idea as to whether we are achieving
this goal.

Priority No. 6 – To be an organization with a clear identity in the community,
important to the communities in which we live, and which will make
these communities a better place because we are there.
This priority is much easier to measure. We contributed materially as a
Grand Lodge to the improvement of our communities through the Masonic
Student Assistance Program, and we let the community know we were there
through our Public Relations Program. The California Masonic Foundation
presented twelve workshops for teachers in California this year, and we trained
567 teachers, counselors, and administrators. We impacted the lives of 7,116
students this year through the Masonic Student Assistance Program. In addition,
we conducted 29 Cornerstone, Dedication, and Mortgage Burning Ceremonies in
local communities, as well as printing and distributing many copies of our Public
Relations Press Kit. We also brought to completion our new public relations
brochure for distribution to the general public and a new brochure for our
Masonic youth groups.

Priority No. 7 – To support the future of our Country by supporting its Youth,
and in particular, by supporting those Masonic Youth Organizations
which make the values of Freemasonry relevant to the next generation
of Americans, and which will also provide the leadership for
Freemasonry in the years to come.
Support of our Masonic Youth was a particular priority and was given
special attention. Twelve thousand dollars was donated to our Masonic Youth
Organizations for leadership training, not only by the California Masonic
Foundation, but also through the Grand Master’s Baja California cruise, which
netted $2,000 for youth leadership training. In addition, contributions to the
Grand Master’s Special Project Fund resulted in more than $45,000 being
contributed to the California Masonic Foundation for scholarships for graduating

50

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

high school seniors. In October 2000, 63 new DeMolays were initiated at the
Grand Master’s DeMolay Class in Northern California.

Priority No. 8 – To increase awareness of Freemasonry in all our communities
by increasing informed and interested public attendance at Grand Lodge
events was added to our list by Deputy Grand Master, Right Worshipful
C. Ray Whitaker. This year we developed the plans which should result
in the implementation of this important goal during our next Grand
Lodge year.

Priority No. 9 – This priority is still in the formative stage. It was added by
Senior Grand Warden, Right Worshipful Bill Holsinger, and reads, “To
reach out to Freemasons who are not actively involved in their lodges,
whether California Masons or sojourners from other jurisdictions, so
that they may once more become active and committed participants in
the future of Freemasonry in California. We have already asked for
lists of sojourning Masons from other jurisdictions as the basis for
asking them to visit our lodges and have received lists containing 7,363
names of sojourning brothers. Working through our Membership
Committee, this information will be forwarded to the closest lodge to
the brother’s residence for follow-up. You will hear more about this
special project in the future.

Themes and Goals
For many years each Grand Master has chosen a theme and direction
for his year. My theme this year was “Freemasonry: Cornerstone of the New
Millennium” and “Beginning today, we’re building for tomorrow.”
As the Grand Lodge has traveled around the jurisdiction, I have talked
about new and interesting programs in our lodges to improve the attendance for
events. Programs such as Child I.D. provide you with an opportunity to
distribute literature about Freemasonry and encourage people in the community
to join with us. I have encouraged our brethren to get involved with Youth
Orders to make them stronger and better. As we all know, these young people
are our future leaders.
How have my efforts been accepted? We are now experiencing
candidates in lodges that have not had degrees in years. There is enthusiasm
among our members when the Grand Lodge has visited for an event. We have
had full to over-flowing sidelines at many events this year. I feel our fraternity is
turning the corner and starting to move in a more positive direction. The Junior
and Senior Warden Retreats are training officers how to plan and perform

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

51

quality events.
Our Executive Committee is committed to continuity in the direction
planned for this Grand Lodge. I wish for them the best as they assume the
leadership of our Grand Lodge.

State of the Craft
It continues to concern me that our membership numbers are not
increasing. As I stated before, several lodges are experiencing positive growth.
Until our membership understands that to experience growth each Mason must
become involved in the process. Members do not come from our sidelines.
They are the result of programs in the communities our lodges serve. Until that
public knows who we are and what we do, I personally do not feel our decline
can be stopped. However, as I traveled around, it became very evident the
younger men are working in the lodges as officers. They are all dedicated to the
ritual of our Grand Lodge. With the leadership training at our retreats, these
men are dedicated to the principles of Freemasonry.

Director of Development
This year the Board of Trustees of the Masonic Homes of California, in
cooperation with the Board of Trustees of the California Masonic Foundation
and the Board of Trustees of the California Masonic Memorial Temple,
authorized us to seek a “Director of Development” to serve all three of these
entities in the creation of a program to ensure stable funding for the future.
These boards are responsible for the major Masonic charities of our Grand
Lodge. The first, and foremost, of course, is the Masonic Homes of California,
and its mission to serve our brethren, and their wives and widows, as well as to
care for the children at our Masonic Home for Children at Covina. You are
aware, also, that the California Masonic Foundation is responsible for our
scholarship program, and for the Masonic Student Assistance Program. You
may not be as aware that the California Masonic Memorial Temple is
responsible for our Grand Lodge Library & Museum, and potentially for our
leadership training programs and our public relations programs as well. All
these require an organized effort to assist our members in understanding the
mission of these charities, and affording them an opportunity to give the kind of
financial support that they need.
To achieve this mission these three boards have asked Grand Lodge to
create the position of Director of Development, a man who would coordinate all
our fund raising efforts by Grand Lodge and its charities, and bring order out of
the less than satisfactory methods we presently use. Many of our brethren are
capable of supporting these charities in a significant way, and asking for their
participation will mean that stable funding for the future will be ensured. You

52

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

will learn more about this new and significant project over the next few months.
Grand Lodge Per Capita Tax
For many years the per capita tax has been held to a 4% increase. I
would love to stand here before you and tell you that per capita will remain the
same or be lowered. That is simply not going to happen in the near future. As
long as we have a declining membership and increasing costs, there will be an
increase in per capita.
I will assure you that steps have been taken by your Executive
Committee to control the budget expenditures. As the costs of living have risen
in our daily lives, so has the cost of doing business in this Grand Lodge and in
the services and programs provided our Craft lodges.
As of June 30, 2001, there were 86,520 Master Masons in good
standing in the Grand Lodge. As of October 8, 2001, there were 378 lodges,
excluding the five research lodges.
I was pleased to constitute Masonic Origins Research Lodge in Clovis
on October 31, 2000.
PanAmerican 2000 Masonic Club continues to function under a letter of
“Approval of Masonic Club Leading to Dispensation of a Lodge” authorized by
Most Worshipful Alvin J. Weis. It is anticipated this club will request a
dispensation for a lodge during the next Grand Lodge year.
Our losses for the last two years have remained constant. For the Grand
Lodge membership year ending June 2000, our loss was 5,552 members. For the
year ending June 2001, our loss was 5,578 members. Many of you in this room
are either officers or Past Masters of your lodges. If I were to ask those who
have signed a petition this year to raise your hands, how many of you could say
that you have recommended a man to become a Mason?
I propose a very simple solution to our membership problem. Each one
of us should replace ourselves during the 2001/2002 Grand Lodge Year. Even
as busy as I have been as Grand Master, I have personally signed three petitions
for membership.
I want you to think about this solution and leave here with a firm
resolve to follow my lead. You can make a difference.
This means that only with a dramatic increase in membership will per
capita ever level out and/or decline.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

53

The major items covered by per capita are:
§

Insurance for Craft Lodges, including property and liability
insurance, and state-required workers’ compensation
insurance;

§

Fraternal Expenses, including the Annual Communication,
Grand Lodge Officers’ travel expense, and Ritual Committee
travel expense;

§

Grand Lodge Office;

§

Grand Lodge Committee expenses; and

§

Printing and distribution of the California Freemason
magazine.

Budget Results
During my year, it has become more apparent in traveling this
jurisdiction that the cost of travel and food expense is going up. All of the
officers of this Grand Lodge shop the market to be as cost effective as possible.
It is a difficult task to travel to various events without incurring some expenses.
Expense Under-runs for 2000-2001 – When you approved this year’s
budget, we planned to have an excess of revenues over expenses of $126,000, of
which $58,000 was to be added to the Grand Lodge Endowment Fund. I am
pleased to report that we currently project that our actual results should be
somewhat better than this $136,000 “bottom line” budget number.

Rose Parade Float
th

On January 1, 2001, the 112 Tournament of Roses took place in
Pasadena, California and the “Family of Freemasonry” was a participant in this
spectacular event. Over one million people lined Colorado Boulevard and
approximately one-half billion people around the world watched on television.
Our float entitled “Let Freedom Ring” passed in review. The theme for
the parade was “The Fabric of America” and our float depicted a Constitution
scroll and Liberty Bell.
I extend my sincere thanks to the three Youth Groups, DeMolay,
Rainbow and Job’s Daughters, for their participation in this project.
Through the generous support of the members of this Grand Lodge, this
worthwhile project was achieved. Additional support also came from our
Concordant and Appendant Bodies and our Youth Orders. Our Job’s Daughters,
Rainbow for Girls and DeMolay donated thousands of hours of labor to decorate

54

PROCEEDINGS OF THE
th

st

the float from December 13 to December 31 . I want to express my personal
thanks to our Youth Groups for their dedication to the “Family of Freemasonry”
float.
Since contributions from other Masonic jurisdictions have diminished
over the years, and it has become increasingly difficult to raise the funds
necessary for this project, it was decided by our Executive Committee to
discontinue this project after the 2001 Float.
The Rose Parade float has been a rewarding experience for our Grand
Lodge. But as with all projects, interest had fallen to a level that we could no
longer sustain the financial obligation for a float beyond 2001.
I wish to extend my personal thanks to Most Worshipful Stanley L.
Channon and his committee for the many years of effort they have expended on
the fraternity’s behalf. Your dedication to this project will long be remembered.

Public and Community Relations
Website Post Card – a full color 4x6 postcard. The facing side reads,
“Find out who we are and what we do.” The website is advertised. The reverse
side gives general membership information.
Ten thousand postcards were printed and approximately 6,500 have
been distributed.
Internet – The Internet has been identified as our most important
public relations tool by every study we have done and every consultant we have
engaged. This has been verified in the fact that we have received more than 250
membership inquiries from our website this year. Therefore, a good portion of
the public relations resources has been dedicated to this endeavor.
The result is a new public website, www.californiamasons.org. This
contemporary site has been designed for the 30-50 year old male who is
interested in Masonry and the several programs and philanthropies of the
fraternity. The website provides information on the values, history and
philanthropy of the organization. Also provided is information about how to
obtain membership in the organization. The website also prominently features
the Scottish Rite Language Disorders Centers including a link to a national
center map.
Those who visit the website can also complete a request form to receive
more information about the fraternity and its several programs.
Media Relations – On-going media relations are an essential element
to our public relations programs. Each month, the Grand Lodge distributes press
releases, requests for coverage and media announcements concerning programs
of the Grand Lodge and its several entities.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

55

Interviews are scheduled with Masonic leaders and the media.
Together with the press kit, our media relations have produced a large number of
well-crafted newspaper articles, radio spots and television news coverage.
Press Kit – a fourteen page document contained in a two-color pocket
folder that is intended to shape the several forms of media within our media
relations program. The kit contains information about the history and origin of
the organization, allied organizations, like the Scottish Rite, and provides
information on the Scottish Rite Language Disorders Centers.
The kit has been very successful in influencing the information
presented in newspapers, radio and television. In many cases, journalists and
copywriters are using the language verbatim from the press kit. The result has
been higher quality and more favorable reporting of the organization.
Fifteen hundred press kits have been printed and approximately 1,200
have been distributed.
Advertising – The first phase of the advertising campaign was intended
to focus on developing a community-based awareness of Masonry. Twenty-six
paid advertisements were placed in newspapers in conjunction with particular
Grand Lodge events and programs. Advertisements included facts about the
event, plus a promotion of the website and general membership information.

Future Public Relations Program
Funds available for the public relations program in this Grand Lodge
year will be directed to furthering our efforts in all of the areas above as well as
the following:

1.

Develop public relations exhibits and displays to be used as public
events and in conjunction with the Child ID Program at several large
venues including the five largest fairs in California.

2.

Move to phase two of the advertising campaign that is designed to
publish general image advertising in large markets.

3.

Develop two new print pieces and replenish the inventory of existing
materials.

Summary
The Grand Lodge of California has gained a lot of experience in the
area of public relations since the beginning of our concentrated efforts in 1999
under the direction of Most Worshipful Melville H. Nahin. Since that time, we
have created a good number of the tools necessary to implement an effective

56

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

public relations effort.
The program has benefited from a consistent effort to build upon the
past successes and a concerted effort to continually explore new avenues for
public exposure.
The ultimate success of the program is contingent upon continued
funding from sources other than the revenue generated from regular per capita
assessments by the Grand Lodge.

Acknowledgements
As my year as Grand Master concludes, it seems that, to paraphrase
Winston Churchill, “Never has one man owed so much to so many.” It is simply
impossible for me to thank everyone to whom I am indebted, but, I must say
thank you to Worshipful Jim Secord, Master of Upland-Mt. Baldy Lodge, my
lodge. I am very proud to be a member of this lodge.
Last year, under the watchful eye of Worshipful Van Owen, a major
restoration was started and it was completed for my first visit back as Grand
Master. I personally thank Worshipful Van and all the members of my lodge
who restored our building.
I will always remember the reception, the Opening of Grand Lodge and
Cornerstone Ceremony, and the combined District reception. I will always
cherish your loyalty and friendship.
I extend my sincere thanks to the elected and appointed officers that
served with me during the 2000-2001 Masonic year. They have traveled around
this great state, attending scheduled events wherever possible. They took these
opportunities to visit with our brethren in attendance. We all tried to be
receptive to our brethren and by sharing thoughts, I hope our lodges became
stronger. I am proud of my selected corps of officers and feel they have served
you in the finest possible manner. I’m so glad I had the privilege of serving with
them.
The office of Grand Master carries with it enormous responsibilities
and it is impossible to be successful without the support of a knowledgeable
office staff. Our Grand Secretary, John Cooper, and his outstanding staff have
supported me in the performance of every daily task, the large and the small. To
the Assistant Grand Lecturers and District Inspectors, our Board members and
committeemen, the Masters and Wardens, and to all the Brethren of this
jurisdiction, I express my profound thanks for a job well done. All of you have
made my year a very rewarding and enjoyable experience. Our travels together
will live in my memory forever.
I could not have completed this experience without the support of my
wife, Nancy. She is truly my best friend and those of you that have met her

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

57

know she is a very competent and organized person. We have been married for
39 years and I can truthfully say she is “The Wind Beneath My Wings.” She has
represented all of you with great dignity wherever we have traveled. Many of
you have heard her speak, and I’m sure you’ll agree with me that she brings an
informative message to the ladies of this jurisdiction. I thank her for making my
year a pleasant and rewarding experience.

Conclusion
As I conclude my year, I have always been guided by the wise counsel
of our Heavenly Father. I began my year filled with high hopes and great
expectations. I hope I have not let you down. I’ve tried my best at all times to
do the things I should and have always kept the welfare of the lodges and the
brethren first in view. My responsibilities and resulting decisions were not taken
lightly.
St. Paul had this to say when he came to the end of his day: “The time
of my departure is at hand – I have fought the good fight, I have finished the
course, I have kept the faith.” End quote. I have tried with all my heart and soul
to be a good Grand Master; kind – fair and friendly. I must now leave my
conduct and actions to your judgment and, if in future years you think of me,
remember my goal as your Grand Master was to bring our fraternity to the
st
forefront of the 21 Century. Our future in this New Millennium lies in the ideas
and programs of those who will assume this chair.

Respectfully submitted,
David C. Decker, Grand Master

NAVIGATION TIPS

Scroll through the document using
scroll bars on the side of the window or
the page navigation tool at the bottom.

Click on any of the items in the bookmark
section of the document and you’ll navigate
directly to that page or section.
You can then move to the next page using the
ARROW KEYS on your keyboard.
Return to the beginning of the document at any
time by clicking on the Table of Contents
Bookmark.
If you want to enlarge or reduce the size of the
document, click on the Zoom In/Zoom Out
bookmarks.
Navigate through the document
by clicking on any of the items in
the Table of Contents or the
Index.
The “Hand Tool” will change to
an “Index Finger”…click on the
line and you’ll move to the
desired page.
To get back, click on the Table
of Contents/Index bookmarks.
